The PLA also prohibits using Marketplace Content to develop, train, enhance, tune, provide source material for or promote generative-AI tools. Music/voice restrictions remain distinct from server-content ownership. This is the current Creator PLA's scope, not a general rule for unrelated platforms or Avocado commerce.

Rockstar's [Mod Guidelines](https://www.rockstargames.com/de/community-resources/mod-guidelines?pubDate=20260921), shown in Rockstar's German-localized page with a September 10, 2026 effective/update date, further state that multiplayer modding is limited to its officially licensed Creator Platform or another direct Rockstar license; its guidelines themselves are **not** a license. The rules prohibit combining Rockstar IP across titles, modifying/reusing Rockstar voiceover recordings, and using a real person's voice/image/likeness without written permission. They also say unofficial projects cannot suggest Rockstar sponsorship, and restrict commercial exploitation to Rockstar-authorized digital distribution under the Creator PLA. This policy summary is useful for discovery, but the guideline page expressly points back to the agreement for actual permission.

Avocado candidate: if an approved creator marketplace ever exists, separate platform approval from endorsement, operator identity/disclaimer, underlying rights, permitted monetization, per-asset license and term, creator technical support, platform transaction support, and service-continuity exposure. Put help/report/bug actions at the point where the user encounters the problem, and show which system owns the ticket; never route users to a community chat as the only support path without an explicit product decision. Verify age/eligibility by the actual product/community authority, not by analogy to a server rule. NoPixel/Rockstar account, Creator PLA service, server, GTA VI runtime, marketplace, Avocado creator product or monetization was accessed or changed.

## 159. 追加調査：creatorの新ルールと旧規約/Privacy Noticeの更新日・対象範囲を揃えて読む（2026-10-18 / RQ14、privacy provenance、creator support）

NoPixel's live [Rulebook](https://www.nopixel.net/rules) says it was updated September 7, 2026 and gives a NoPixel V-specific support path through the Hub, pause menu, `/bug` and `/report`. The separately opened [Terms](https://www.nopixel.net/terms-of-service) and [Privacy Notice](https://www.nopixel.net/privacy-notice) each still show a December 27, 2023 update date; their scope text describes independent GTA V roleplay servers, Steam/Rockstar IDs, priority-access transactions and related processing. The Privacy Notice enumerates identifiers including account/server IDs, approximate location for server selection, transaction IDs, and gameplay activity used for analytics, moderation, fraud prevention and security. It says date of birth is gathered solely to check 18+ eligibility, but the policy itself labels this minimum age for its covered legacy servers. The website Terms also say Primary Server access is invitation/application-based and monthly Priority Access only improves the queue without guaranteeing entry at any time. Do not assume these 2023 legacy legal texts fully document NoPixel V's 2026 service, current enrollment or live privacy implementation; the current rulebook, legal documents and Rockstar's Creator PLA are separate sources with separate scope and effective/update dates.

Avocado candidate: surface each policy's owner, last-updated/effective date, covered service/data flow and source; flag a newer operational rule alongside older service terms for legal review instead of merging them. For a real product, disclose each data category with its specific purpose and service/vendor boundary (identity linking, age eligibility, moderation evidence, coarse location for routing, transaction reconciliation), retention and correction/appeal route; collect and retain only the minimum needed for the stated purpose. Show the difference between an invitation/application decision and paid queue priority, and never describe paid priority as a guaranteed session or successful task. These are evidence-driven review candidates, not legal advice or Avocado age, collection, retention, pay-to-prioritize, moderation or identity policy changes.

## 157. 追加調査：refund eventとentitlement失効は同じ状態遷移ではない（2026-10-18 / SIM/eSIM、RQ15、ledger reconciliation）

Microsoft's [GDK clawback guidance](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/gaming/gdk/docs/store/commerce/service-to-service/xstore-managing-refunds-and-chargebacks?view=gdk-2604) gives the platform-specific service-side detail behind the general cancellation comparison in §149. Its queue distinguishes return/refund events from payment-institution chargebacks and chargeback reversals. For game/durable items, Microsoft documents different effects: `Returned`/`Revoked` remove entitlement/license, `Refunded` can leave entitlement in place while the event is logged and monitored, and `ChargebackReversal` restores a previously removed entitlement. Bundle events are routed to queues associated with configured product/client identifiers; bundle and included-product events can have different mappings. These are Xbox GDK rules and event contracts, not universal commerce semantics.

Avocado candidate: keep provider order/payment events, the product's contractual entitlement decision, service activation/suspension, consumed usage, and any compensating ledger entry as linked but distinct records. Match provider events idempotently by the provider's stable transaction/product identifiers; distinguish refund, return, chargeback, reversal and disputed/pending states; make the policy-controlled access change auditable and user-visible. Never infer that “refunded” necessarily means “access revoked,” or the reverse; the local contract, source event and settled state decide. This is a review input only: no Microsoft partner integration, clawback queue, Avocado Wallet ledger or entitlement policy was implemented.

## 160. 追加調査：Cfxはサーバー検索用metadataをPortalへ移し、タグを5個に制限予定（2026-10-18 / RQ14、catalog migration/discovery）

Cfx's [October 14, 2026 Portal announcement](https://forum.cfx.re/t/portal-update-server-metadata-management/5431315) says server names, descriptions, tags, languages and images are moving from locally edited server configuration files to the Cfx Portal. It backfills recently found values; absent metadata remains blank until the owner fills it and the public directory then explicitly says “Missing server data (must be configured in Portal).” Search tags will be owner-set through Portal but capped at five unique values; if a listing has more, only the first five chronologically remain visible. Banner/profile image guidance is normalized, and server owners can add an associated Discord URL in the details area. The announcement says most changes take effect next week, October 21; this is announced future state, not a verified completed rollout.

Avocado candidate: when changing a creator/Tool listing from decentralized or repo-local metadata to a canonical portal, inventory what can be backfilled, which fields need owner action, and how missing/unverified values appear before and after migration. Separate searchable controlled tags from display names/free description, language support from market availability, and linked community channel from official support. Preview the actual directory card and fallback state before publication; cap/fix tag vocabulary only where search quality requires it. Cfx's rollout is specific to FiveM/RedM server discovery; no Avocado catalog, portal or tagging policy changed.

## 161. 追加調査：Cfx Marketplaceで「verified creator」と個々のresource/compatibilityを別に表示（2026-10-18 / RQ14、marketplace trust/creator onboarding）

The live [Cfx Marketplace](https://marketplace.cfx.re/) labels itself as the Rockstar modding UGC marketplace for **RedM and FiveM** and categorizes listings (maps, scripts, character, clothing, vehicles, etc.). An opened [creator profile](https://marketplace.cfx.re/creators/965175-electus-scripts) displays “Verified Asset Creator,” a unique-download count, external social and customer-support links, report path, and filters for game platform, framework, source availability, price, purchase type and single-vs-bundle. An opened [Electus Computers product detail](https://marketplace.cfx.re/packages/7728246-electus-computers) shows a product title, seller, price, stated FiveM platform, framework support, detailed seller-written feature claims, technical dependencies, optional integrations, admin permissions, localization, documentation and Discord support. It also claims an in-world computer OS spanning laptops/desktops/TVs, persistent files/settings, SIM/network accessories, 40+ apps and an API for third-party apps. This is an individual seller's listing, not proof that features were independently tested or verified, not an official GTA VI capability, and not a rights/safety warranty. Cfx's Marketplace report link provides a separate compliance reporting route; the presence of “Cyberpunk” in another listing title does not by itself establish infringement or compliance.

Avocado candidate: label what a badge actually verifies (creator identity/status, if defined) independently of exact package compatibility, code/data safety, authorship/rights, installation result or benchmark. Show framework/platform/version, hard versus optional dependencies, source/escrow status, support owner, purchase type, localization and report path before approval; treat download counts as popularity only. The computer/phone/SIM/app-store concept is an adjacent creator-market research lead for testing whether developers want extensible in-world software interfaces—not validated demand for Avocado, GTA VI, or the physical SIM/eSIM product. Any future product idea requires separate rights, platform eligibility and demand proof; no code, art, listing, account or integration was copied or used.

## 162. 追加調査：FiveMのseller listingはlive auction・player ownership・phone/tabletをつなぐが、GTA VIの機能証拠ではない（2026-10-18 / RQ14、RQ13 boundary、workflow analogy）

An opened Cfx Marketplace [used-vehicle resource listing](https://marketplace.cfx.re/packages/7588829-jg-used-vehicles) describes seller-claimed listings bound to player-owned vehicles, showing vehicle mileage/condition/modifications and owner count; fixed prices, offers/counteroffers, auctions with reserve/buy-now/deadlines, contact-only adverts, integrated dealer balances, phone/tablet access, active listing limits, buyer-visible photos and “verified reviews.” These are described features of a paid third-party FiveM resource, not verified Rockstar services or GTA VI functionality; no actual transaction/review system was operated. It resembles marketplace/workflow patterns, but cannot be treated as authority for converting GTA VI assets or game currency into real money; Rockstar's Creator PLA separately restricts cash-out, game-currency sales and selling Rockstar-created virtual items.

Another paid [FiveM listing](https://marketplace.cfx.re/packages/7209027-live-facial-recognition) advertises fictional in-server recognition cameras, wanted-state flags, notes, framework/role permissions and client-local persistent captures. This listing is not evidence of Rockstar/Avocado biometric capability, nor does `client-local` alone prove a privacy or safety guarantee. Its value is a security review prompt: for any future Avocado identity/media feature, document the real subject/data type, permitted principal and purpose, storage location, notice, retention/deletion, accuracy/error handling and reporting route; never borrow in-game police surveillance as a product feature by implication.

Avocado candidate: where a future already-approved marketplace or task workflow exists, keep quote, hold/escrow, seller authority, asset provenance, transfer status, cancellation, dispute/report and confirmed settlement as explicit states and reuse existing `lib/workflow.ts`/Wallet contracts. “Verified review” belongs only to a reviewer with verified acquisition/use; a seller's marketing label, public listing or in-game role is not proof of ownership, legal right, install success or quality. Phone/tablet reachability should reuse the user's supported client where authorized and describe shared state/support owner across surfaces. No Avocado marketplace, review, biometric, game-asset, Wallet or real-money conversion schema changed.

## 163. 追加調査：「AI Assistant」の製品ラベルと実際のモデル/権限を分ける（2026-10-18 / RQ14、AI06、security boundary）

Rockstar's [GTA Online mansion announcement](https://www.rockstargames.com/newswire/article/51358a55o2o11o/gta-online-a-safehouse-in-the-hills-coming-december-10) calls the mansion feature a state-of-the-art “AI Assistant” for professional and personal tasks. Rockstar's [Title Update 1.72 notes](https://support.rockstargames.com/articles/0ExWSr9Bvq5Putzsn5w54/gtav-title-update-1-72-notes-ps5-ps4-xbox-series-x-or-xbox-one-pc-enhanced) describe its actual listed actions as temporarily boosting one business's production, fast-travelling to owned properties/businesses, and hosting a mansion party; users choose one of three personas (Angel, Haviland, OG), and the assistant is reachable through a phone contact in Freemode. The official page does not identify an LLM, generative model, cloud provider, or arbitrary action execution. These are GTA Online facts, not evidence about GTA VI.

Rockstar's same update introduces a [Mission Creator](https://www.rockstargames.com/newswire/article/51358a55o2o11o/gta-online-a-safehouse-in-the-hills-coming-december-10) for designing and publishing missions with configurable mechanics, objectives and placeable actors; five example missions are editable, per Rockstar's support notes. The [official patch history](https://support.rockstargames.com/articles/0ExWSr9Bvq5Putzsn5w54/gtav-title-update-1-72-notes-ps5-ps4-xbox-series-x-or-xbox-one-pc-enhanced) records concrete post-launch corrections: team test cases failing immediately, object/model placement and deletion errors, mission flags applied at the wrong point, actor hacking/position behavior, settings persisting between creator launches, and objective/loot/invite/player-count issues. This is useful evidence that a creator tool needs a repeatable test/publish loop, state reset and correction path; it does not prove GTA VI has such a toolkit or that any Avocado creator product is needed.

A third-party [Cfx forum release](https://forum.cfx.re/t/mad-goon-ai-concierge-interaction/5377166) advertises a separate FiveM add-on that semantically matches input to recorded GTA Online concierge voice lines offline. This is only the seller/developer's claim, not a tested benchmark or Rockstar confirmation; the use of game voice material also raises the distinct Creator PLA limits recorded in §158 and is not an implementation recommendation. Together the examples show that the marketing label “AI” does not tell a user whether intent is matched against fixed choices, generated locally, sent to a remote model, or allowed to trigger actions.

Avocado candidate: describe the actual mechanism, inputs/data sent, dependency/network state and available actions; keep persona/tone separate from capability and authority; route model/planner output through the established Broker permission, identity and approval boundary. For an authoring workflow, let creators start from known examples, validate before publish, identify the test environment and reset state between runs, surface failures and rollback/revision status, and distinguish content approval from technical compatibility. These observations belong to GTA Online and a third-party FiveM release, not GTA VI evidence; no assistant, model, mission creator, creator approval process, or execution permission was added or changed in Avocado.

## 164. 追加調査：公式Mission Creatorの学習・試作・推薦・執行は独立した段階（2026-10-18 / RQ14、creator lifecycle）

Rockstar's [Community Mission Creator guide](https://www.rockstargames.com/newswire/article/39a3412379o79o/play-thrilling-new-gta-online-experiences-built-by-the-community-with?pubDate=20260430) describes a graduated flow: five playable/editable Rockstar examples teach the editor; authors can share a working prototype in the official Discord creator channels for feedback; they then publish in game and optionally tag `#communityseries` for possible editorial consideration; players can bookmark jobs and send experience feedback through Rockstar's feedback portal. Rockstar separately curates and features selected creations, and its announcement attaches a one-time in-game award to creators whose work is featured. The channels and incentives are specific to GTA Online, PS5/Xbox Series/PC Enhanced availability, and the date-specific announcement.

Rockstar Support's [Mission Creator unavailable article](https://support.rockstargames.com/articles/1lmcf3ZEFlNIYlOhwDpY6z/grand-theft-auto-online-mission-creator-unavailable) states that prior Community Guidelines/Terms violations may temporarily disable Creator access, new violations may lead to suspensions/bans, and an enforcement action can be appealed for reevaluation. Its separate [Mission Creator patch history](https://support.rockstargames.com/articles/0ExWSr9Bvq5Putzsn5w54/gtav-title-update-1-72-notes-ps5-ps4-xbox-series-x-or-xbox-one-pc-enhanced) documents ongoing fixes to test starts, actors, object placement/deletion, objective completion, saved creator settings, invites and player count. Therefore “created,” “tested,” “published,” “eligible for nomination,” “editorially featured,” “policy-enforced,” and “under appeal” describe different states; a prototype/review/feature label alone is not a runtime, quality, rights or safety certification.

Avocado candidate: if RQ14's existing author workflow is extended, provide guided editable examples, a private/sandbox test, voluntary peer feedback, explicit publish and an opt-in discovery signal; show the review/featured decision separately from user ratings, compatibility checks and rights/safety review. For enforcement, identify the restricted action and current scope, make temporary suspension/expiry distinct from permanent removal, preserve a direct appeal/re-evaluation route and display status changes. Test state resets and regressions across the actual supported task fixture; don't import GTA Online's specific award, moderation thresholds, appeal standard or content policy. These are GTA Online observations only, not GTA VI creator support, Avocado policy or validated demand; no implementation or enforcement decision changed.

The Rockstar [1.73 patch notes (October 1, 2026)](https://support.rockstargames.com/articles/4vRqEDvjUs9h7nqRUgc8YO/gtav-title-update-1-73-notes-ps5-ps4-xbox-series-x-or-xbox-one-pc-enhanced-legacy) further say that a player may nominate up to 16 Player-Created Jobs from the end-of-mission leaderboard or Pause/Recently Played/Bookmarked/My Jobs menus; after a user-created Job, some nominated jobs by people in the current lobby appear on the Next Job vote screen. This is a narrow, contextual discovery queue backed by player nomination and immediate group vote, not a global quality ranking or guarantee that a nominated Job is shown. If an Avocado directory ever supports suggestions, distinguish a user's explicit nomination, eligible current context, candidate set, actual exposure and subsequent selection; record who controls each step and allow users to understand why an item was suggested. Do not copy the “16” limit or game-context rules into Avocado.

## 165. 追加調査：creator向けAIは役割期待が割れるので、提案の主導権と編集権を計測（2026-10-18 / AI06、RQ14）

The CHI 2019 paper [Friend, Collaborator, Student, Manager](https://arxiv.org/abs/1901.06417) presents two mixed-methods studies (over 100 participants in total) of Morai Maker, a turn-based AI-assisted editor for Super Mario Bros.-style levels. Participants differed in the role they wanted the AI to play, summarized by the authors as friend, collaborator, student or manager, and also changed how they designed in response to the AI/tool. The authors report no single consistently superior agent; in the initial study participants deleted an average of 60% of the AI's additions. The second, qualitative study contacted 24 practicing designers, 16 participated, and two sessions crashed due to networking issues, leaving 14; the paper cautions against strong quantitative interpretation. These findings are about a research prototype and do not evaluate Rockstar Mission Creator, GTA VI, contemporary foundation models or Avocado.

Avocado candidate: in any separately authorized creator experiment, test discrete opt-in assistance modes such as “suggest”, “co-edit” and “review/teach” with the action/permission boundary visible; allow accept, revise, undo and dismiss per proposal; log the proposed change and human decision separately. Measure time-to-usable-result, accepted/revised/rejected share, undo/recovery, surprise/control and dependency on the agent by mode and creator experience. Do not infer mode preference from one session or claim that an AI partner necessarily increases creativity. The study supplies a hypothesis and evaluation design, not implementation requirements, product demand, or an excuse to expand the AI's action permissions.

A second primary study, [Beyond Prompts](https://arxiv.org/abs/2305.07465), compared seven mixed-initiative/co-creative story-writing interfaces with 185 participants. In this bounded task, conditions covering more communication types tended to be preferred, preference varied by creative/AI experience, and participants asked for “scrutability”—a way to inspect or probe how the model reached an output—and explanation. Its prototype used GPT-J for story generation plus a separate critique model; the paper notes difficulty learning global operations during a short (<30-minute) session and calls for follow-up work. Avocado candidate: compare user-led prompt, per-item suggestion, structural/whole-task reflection, and local/global scope in the actual permitted workflow; expose the model request/result and rationale/evidence that is genuinely available, then measure learnability as well as outcome. This story-writing study is not GTA/Avocado evidence, does not justify offering every mode, and is not a requirement to expose private chain-of-thought; no model, telemetry, workflow or permission changed.

## 166. 追加調査：購入者レビュー・不正通報・返金サポートは別の窓口と証拠（2026-10-18 / RQ14、trust/support）

Sony's current [US PlayStation Ratings and Reviews Policy](https://www.playstation.com/en-us/legal/reviews-policy/) says a review is available only for a digital game acquired via a PlayStation account, at one review per product/account. Reviews are public and include the account identity; Sony says it does not filter only positive feedback, does not pay for reviews (disclosing any incentive), and treats positive/negative reviews equally. A separate report button captures a reason/details for moderation. The same policy says the reviews surface is generally not monitored for technical support or refund requests and routes those issues to help pages. A third-party provider authenticates reviews; violation may remove review/rating or trigger account action. The policy says regional/product availability may vary and is Sony US platform policy, not Rockstar's creator rules or Avocado policy.

Avocado candidate: bind each public review to the precise item/revision and only expose a “purchased” claim when an acquisition record supports it; label actual install/use/compatibility evidence separately because a purchase alone proves neither. Mark paid/incentivized reviews if such a future flow is approved; separate public opinion/reputation from private report, refund, support case and technical acceptance. Route reports to a defined moderation owner with case status and response/appeal, while support/refund routes remain seller/provider-owned and visible. This is a PlayStation policy example; no Avocado review, badge, moderation, support or refund flow changed.

## 167. 追加調査：contextual AIは送信元ごとの選択と状態表示を測る（2026-10-18 / AI06、MAT18、privacy boundary）

The CHI 2024 paper [ContextCam](https://doi.org/10.1145/3613904.3642129) studies an image co-creation prototype that used contextual inputs such as location, weather, facial expression, music and phone-screen content to propose creative themes. A formative study interviewed 23 image-generation users; a follow-up tested 16 participants in real settings for three days and analyzed 136 scenarios. Participants chose a system-suggested theme in 92.9% of those scenarios and gave high enjoyment/engagement ratings, but the main study was small, was preceded by a 30-minute tutorial, and concerned image creation rather than Avocado jobs. Participants varied in which sources they used; some later turned off location and screen-content inputs after learning the system. The paper reported ethical review and informed consent. This is not evidence that Avocado should collect ambient, location, camera, microphone or screen data.

Avocado candidate: if a future task expressly benefits from user context, test a source-by-source disclosure/selector tied to the current job (e.g. selected document, connected service, location if specifically needed); show what will be used/sent before execution, offer no-context and per-source off paths, and preserve regular task use when context is declined. Measure correct understanding, task quality, unwanted context, opt-out, recovery and burden over repeated use. Never infer authorization from context or use location/voice/screen presence as identity, approval or permission. This is an optional UI research hypothesis only; no data collection, sensor use, consent, model integration or policy was changed.

## 168. 追加調査：「GTA VI風」FiveM商品は公式仕様でなく販売者自身の想像と明示（2026-10-18 / RQ14、catalog claim provenance）

A Cfx forum [paid FiveM HUD listing titled “GTA VI–Inspired Interface”](https://forum.cfx.re/t/hud-gta-vi-inspired-interface-for-fivem/5371242) advertises an editable HUD and describes its GTA VI styling. After a forum user asked what evidence supported that visual claim, the seller replied that it could be rumors or their own vision and “Nobody knows.” The same listing separately describes concrete FiveM functions (cash/bank, job, voice and vehicle indicators, layout editing), states ESX/QB-Core/QBox dependencies, and links a $16 Tebex purchase. This is a seller-authored listing and forum discussion, not Rockstar UI documentation, a compatibility test, verified sales/demand, or evidence the product actually matches GTA VI. Cfx's category and the listing identify FiveM; do not relabel it a GTA VI add-on.

Avocado candidate: test a provenance-aware listing card that visually distinguishes (1) official/versioned evidence, (2) seller-stated capability, (3) independently reproduced compatibility, and (4) speculative inspiration. Attach each claim to its source, owner and verification date; retain correction/report routes and avoid deriving a product requirement from a speculative title, likes or sales language. The example is useful for marketplace-claim comprehension only; no Avocado catalog, creator badge, moderation or rights policy changed.

## 169. 追加調査：Mission Creatorの再起動間設定漏れ・テスト経路不一致は独立して記録（2026-10-18 / RQ14、creator validation/recovery）

Rockstar Support's [GTAV 1.72 notes](https://support.rockstargames.com/articles/0ExWSr9Bvq5Putzsn5w54/gtav-title-update-1-72-notes-ps5-ps4-xbox-series-x-or-s-xbox-one-pc-enhanced) list Mission Creator issues across patch entries: vehicle weapon, ammo type, auto-critical/aim-assist, range, shooting-range and camera settings persisted across Creator launches; team sizes 2–4 could fail mission testing immediately; a points value could fail to save; objective flags could be applied at the wrong stage; model memory display could be wrong; and objects could disappear after actor deletion. Patch notes document that fixes were released, not the original implementation root causes or a complete regression matrix. These are GTA Online Creator defects; they do not indicate Avocado has similar defects.

Avocado candidate: for any separately approved author/test workflow, make each test's input revision, setup, participant/team configuration and expected starting state reproducible. Run one-case setup and multi-case reuse in sequence, restart between them, then assert that prior settings/results do not leak, saved values survive intended boundaries, previews match actual execution, and reset/recovery is available after failure. Record the exact affected revision and rerun result. Do not infer code-level persistence guarantees from a UI label; no Avocado authoring runtime, test suite or release gate changed.

## 170. 追加調査：GTA Onlineのcontent ratingにゲーム内報酬が付く（2026-10-18 / RQ14、trust signal/incentive disclosure）

The same [Rockstar 1.72 notes](https://support.rockstargames.com/articles/0ExWSr9Bvq5Putzsn5w54/gtav-title-update-1-72-notes-ps5-ps4-xbox-series-x-or-s-xbox-one-pc-enhanced) state that GTA Online players receive GTA$500 in addition to RP for rating content. This proves a game-specific in-world reward attached to a rating action, not a public written review, a positive-only incentive, or an increase/decrease in review integrity; the note does not specify how the rating is displayed or weighted.

Avocado candidate: if a future approved feedback/rating mechanism has any reward, disclose the reward source, eligibility and conditions alongside the contribution; distinguish rewarded feedback from independently submitted opinion and from verified installation/compatibility/use evidence. Test whether users understand what they are being asked to rate and whether incentives alter participation or rating behavior. This GTA Online mechanic does not set Avocado compensation or review policy; none changed.

## 171. 追加調査：作成物の移行はfamily内同期とfamily間コピーで異なる（2026-10-18 / RQ14、artifact revision/portability）

Rockstar Support's [GTAV Enhanced for PC migration FAQ](https://support.rockstargames.com/articles/57ui3EVyqF5iMb0CSguvPm/impact-to-player-created-missions-and-mission-ratings-after-migrating-online) (updated February 27, 2025) says player-created missions are shared between Legacy and Enhanced PC profiles; edits in Legacy appear in Enhanced, but missions created or edited in Enhanced are unavailable in Legacy. Ratings of Rockstar-created and Verified Missions are shared both ways. Its separate [console-family migration FAQ](https://support.rockstargames.com/articles/2kQlhjhwtrHqdcib03p7LR/gta-online-save-migration-impact-on-player-created-missions-and-mission-ratings) (updated April 27, 2024) distinguishes same-family PS4→PS5 or Xbox One→Series X|S mission/rating sharing from cross-family migration, which copies player-created missions without later edit synchronization; the listed copied/shared ratings are specifically Rockstar-created and Verified missions. These are narrow GTA Online migration contracts, not GTA VI cross-save or Avocado behavior.

Avocado candidate: for any supported artifact portability feature, state whether it is a live shared identity, one-time copy, fork, or export; show source revision, target identity, timestamp, edit directionality, conflict behavior and which ratings/verification records (if any) transfer. Verify with a later edit on each side, not just by comparing a migration-time snapshot. Keep the artifact's ownership/provenance record distinct from feedback attached to a platform-specific copy. No Avocado sync, rating or portability contract changed.

## 172. 追加調査：暗号化範囲・編集可能部・顧客側の旧版実行を個別開示（2026-10-18 / RQ14、catalog security/versioning）

Current Cfx [Asset Escrow documentation](https://docs.fivem.net/docs/server-manual/asset-escrow/) says it protects selected Lua/YFT/YDD/YDR files from direct access/modification, but does not support NUI. Creator reupload replaces the prior version in the seller's account, while customers who already downloaded an earlier version can continue using it; expired subscriptions remove the asset and it no longer starts, and escrowed assets are distributed via Tebex. This is a particular platform's package and entitlement behavior, not an Avocado security guarantee or the rule that every marketplace buyer has immediate updates.

A recent seller-authored [RedM crafting resource listing](https://forum.cfx.re/t/interactive-alchemy-craft-table-recipe-discovery-throwable-effects/5430523) illustrates the resulting disclosure surface: it names protected internal Lua, unencrypted NUI JavaScript, editable configuration/locales/framework bridges/CSS, required framework/database dependencies, tested two-player synchronization cases, support owner, approximate package size and an explicit integration it does not provide. A later forum update describes new framework support, custom items, and an instruction to update two modules together while backing up/merging prior configuration. This is one seller's statement, not a reproduced compatibility/security test or GTA VI item.

Avocado candidate: on any separately approved catalog, show protected versus inspectable/editable package components, the actual distribution and purchase channel, seller support boundary, dependency and explicitly unsupported integrations, verification environment and version. On update, state whether seller master, downloadable package, already-installed customer copy, saved user config and companion modules advance together; offer a migration/backup path and a way to identify the running revision. Do not equate encryption/escrow with safe code, rights clearance, compatibility or platform-key signing. No Avocado catalog schema, update policy, encryption or entitlement changed.

## 173. 追加調査：生成AIへの取込みは一般ToS・Creator PLA・MODガイドの適用範囲を分けて審査（2026-10-18 / AI06、RQ14、IP provenance）

The [Japanese Rockstar Terms of Service](https://www.rockstargames.com/jp/legal?country=jp) shows a last-updated date of February 28, 2025. Its section 2.3 says the limited license does not authorize use of the Services (including Content), Virtual Items or an Account to develop, train, enhance, provide source material for, or promote Generative AI Tools, and explicitly defines that tool category broadly. The [Creator Platform License Agreement](https://static.cfx.re/platform-license-agreement-12-jan-2026.pdf) has a distinct Marketplace/Custom Server scope and “Covered Game” framework; prior review did not establish GTA VI as covered. Rockstar's [September 2026 mod guidelines](https://www.rockstargames.com/jp/community-resources/mod-guidelines?pubDate=20260921) separately direct creators back to both ToS and Creator PLA for the governing license and say Rockstar reserves deletion rights. These documents are not interchangeable, and this section is a source/scope observation rather than a legal interpretation of every public announcement, third-party discussion, screenshot or independently created analysis.

Avocado candidate: before a future product trains on, enhances from, or uses media/account/virtual-item data as source material for generative AI, record the exact source, owner, content category, governing agreement/version, covered product/platform, purpose and express permission basis; route ambiguous scope for legal review and provide a no-source-data fallback. Keep public factual metadata/independent commentary, licensed creator assets, user-submitted gameplay media, game captures, account data and in-world items as separately assessed categories rather than assuming one blanket permission. This does not conclude that this research itself has been legally approved or that an Avocado use is permitted; no dataset, training pipeline, game account, scraped corpus or integration was created or changed.

## 174. 追加調査：2026 MODガイドラインはマルチプレイヤー改変・クロス作品IP・音声再利用を制限（2026-10-18 / RQ14、IP/use-channel boundary）

Rockstar's [Japanese MOD Guidelines dated September 10, 2026](https://www.rockstargames.com/jp/community-resources/mod-guidelines?pubDate=20260921) state, among other things: do not expand/alter official story continuity or combine IP between Rockstar titles; multiplayer MOD creation is limited to Rockstar's officially licensed Creator Platform or direct Rockstar license; do not run games on unshipped platforms/hardware; don't reuse Rockstar-published game voiceover recordings or real people's voice/image/likeness without written permission; and monetized MODs must use an approved digital storefront under applicable laws/policies. The same guide says it does not allow real-money gambling/loot boxes/gacha, sale or purchase of in-game virtual currency for real money, crypto/NFT/token use or promotion, third-party advertisements/sponsorship, or charging for access to Rockstar-created game assets/missions/DLC. It says the guide is a summary, not a license, points to the Terms and Creator PLA, and reserves deletion rights. Rockstar support separately directs single-player mods and RP servers to ToS, Creator PLA and Mod Guidelines. This is policy text for MOD/Creator activity; it does **not** establish that GTA VI supports mods, multiplayer Creator services, FiveM, a creator marketplace or any Avocado connection. The current Creator PLA's “Covered Games” must still be checked separately.

Avocado candidate: keep product information, game companion overlays, single-player modification, multiplayer servers, creator marketplace licensing and users' fan media as separate feature/use categories with explicit game, platform, permitted channel, asset/voice rights and written authorization. Keep RQ13 game currency entirely separate from real-money Wallet, and never treat rules for a Rockstar-approved MOD storefront as permission for external asset sales, cash-out, conversion, wagering, randomized purchase or third-party promotion. Don't advertise endorsement, compatibility or permission from an “inspired by” label or Rockstar IP mark. If a proposed workflow depends on unverified GTA VI multiplayer/creator support, show it as unavailable/unverified and gate release until exact Rockstar/platform authorization and technical acceptance exist. This is a requirements-screening hypothesis, not legal advice or an Avocado policy decision; no MOD, integration, asset reuse, promotion or pilot was made.

## 175. 追加調査：公式Music hubはscore、ゲーム内radio preview、album singles、物販を分けている（2026-10-18 / RQ14、catalog media provenance）

Rockstar's live [GTA VI music hub](https://www.rockstargames.com/VI/music) separates an original-score section, radio-station previews, and a separately branded “Grand Theft Auto VI: The Album” area. At the time reviewed it showcased six stations (including Cocoteo, Back Country, Afro Bank FM, The Chamber, Flash FM and Dirty South Classics), says 34 original tracks for the Album, offers nine singles, and separately links vinyl/CD preorders. This is a hand-picked promotional preview and music/merch page; it is not an exhaustive radio playlist or proof all soundtrack album tracks play in-game. Each station card can use fictional DJ/host labels, while album purchases/listening are separately routed. None of this establishes an Avocado right to stream game music, use station artwork, scrape a playlist, or represent a song as licensed for other contexts.

Avocado candidate: when cataloging bundled product/service media, keep interactive/in-product track, original score, promotional single, standalone album, physical variant and sampled preview as distinct media types with separate exact source, rightsholder/license, territory, effective term, playback/download channel and availability. Mark “sampled selection” versus “complete list,” preserve the page's last-verified date, and do not infer a game-use license from a standalone album purchase. This is media-catalog UX research only; no audio was downloaded, embedded, replayed as product media, or added to Avocado.

## 176. 追加調査：Cfxのresource manifestは宣言targetと実行時requirementを別に記述できる（2026-10-18 / RQ14、SDK/catalog compatibility）

The official [Cfx resource-manifest documentation](https://docs.fivem.net/docs/scripting-reference/resource-manifest/resource-manifest/) shows a developer-authored manifest declaring framework version, target games, author, description, version, scripts, and other metadata. Its dependency field can name a resource or impose runtime constraints such as minimum server build, granted policy, OneSync, minimum game build or a required native; exports and `server_only` can also be explicitly represented. The related current [manifest reference](https://docs.fivem.net/docs/scripting-reference/resource-manifest/) says a default/empty manifest version is slated for removal, while the Lua 5.3 enable flag is deprecated since June 2025. A manifest is structured self-declaration/loader configuration, not independent evidence that a release was tested against every listed runtime or that the claims are safe.

Avocado candidate: before any already-approved catalog evolution, compare the existing Tool contract against the distinction between author-declared targets/dependencies, loader-enforced prerequisites, independent validation results, and known deprecation/removal dates. Reuse current schemas and display what the user must install/configure versus what was actually exercised on an identified host/version; reject or flag missing mandatory requirements before execution. Do not copy FiveM names or metadata vocabulary into Avocado, and do not treat manifest/signature as security approval. No schema, catalog, SDK or runtime changed.

## 177. 追加調査：FiveMのsecurity guidanceはclient inputを非信頼としてserver-sideで再検証（2026-10-18 / Security、RQ13 boundary）

Cfx's official [Secure Your Events guide](https://docs.fivem.net/docs/developers/server-security/) warns that a cheating client can trigger client/server events in arbitrary contexts. It recommends registering same-context-only events locally, using network events only where needed, and rechecking money, state, inventory, position, experience and permissions from server-side sources; it says client-side checks can be overridden and server checks improve but do not eliminate risk. The official [TriggerServerEvent reference](https://docs.fivem.net/docs/scripting-reference/runtimes/lua/functions/TriggerServerEvent/) likewise says to validate all client-sent data and keep logic server-side where possible. These are FiveM server engineering rules, not GTA VI protocol details or evidence of GTA VI anti-cheat/API behavior.

Avocado candidate: for an already-authorized client→service action, treat UI/agent/client claims as proposals; bind the authenticated principal server-side, re-evaluate action-scoped policy/entitlement, constrain value/quantity/target, enforce nonce/idempotency and replay/duplicate protection, and record authoritative evidence before ledger/Tool success. For RQ13, game-facing points and balances must remain outside real-money Wallet authority; do not create any currency converter or bridge. This is a cross-check for existing Broker/Wallet contracts, not a new requirement or implementation; no GTA VI/client data was connected.

## 178. 追加調査：Rockstar ToSの遠隔利用・bot・データ収集制限は通常のcloud AIと分けて適用（2026-10-18 / Security、remote access boundary）

The Japanese [Rockstar Terms of Service](https://www.rockstargames.com/jp/legal?country=jp), last updated February 28, 2025, section 6.4 prohibits using the services through remote servers/virtual PCs or other systems that enable downloading/streaming service or content to separate internet-enabled devices, unless Rockstar provides separate express written terms. It separately prohibits using bugs/cheats/mod menus/hacks/scripts/bots/unauthorized modifications or other means that result in ToS violations, including methods to collect information/user data, exploit vulnerabilities, bypass moderation, or disrupt/redirect/interfere with intended service operation. The same Terms have separate ToS limitations on remote/content uses and generative-AI sourcing (§173). This text concerns accessing Rockstar Services and should not be misread as a blanket prohibition on unrelated cloud inference while a player uses a separately authorized game client; the exact proposed product and service path require scope-specific review.

Avocado candidate: before any future game-client companion, remote-play, screen-reading, account-linking, scripted action or server-side integration, map every endpoint, execution location, data captured/sent, service owner, platform rule and explicit written authorization; otherwise keep the product non-connected and limit it to independently sourced facts or user-supplied notes that are permitted for that task. Cloud AI access for RockstarOS/Sky on its own must remain separately described from executing or streaming a Rockstar service. No Rockstar account was accessed, no remote session or bot was used, and no Avocado game integration was added.

## 179. 追加調査：creator人気シグナルはUGC popularityと関連しても品質・安全性とは別（2026-10-18 / RQ14、discovery/trust signal）

Kang, Lee & Lee's peer-reviewed [2024 Entertainment Computing study](https://doi.org/10.1016/j.entcom.2024.100697) analyzed 1,301 Roblox fighting games and creator-profile data for how young users chose user-generated games; using a two-stage least-squares design, the paper reports creator follower counts and limited-item holdings positively associated with game popularity. Its scope is popularity/selection on a specific Roblox genre/platform, not per-item safety, compatibility, originality, usability or outcome quality; the abstract does not warrant carrying effect sizes to other markets. This does not establish that Rockstar or Avocado should use creator popularity as a rank.

Avocado candidate: if an authorized directory shows creator popularity or social proof, label its exact measurement and period and place it apart from item-level moderation, source/rights review, compatibility testing and user outcomes. In a fixture-based study, compare whether users correctly infer “popular creator” versus “this exact item was tested/secure” and whether adding a popularity signal biases use of safer or better-matched results. Keep paid exposure distinct from organic popularity. This is a hypothesis from one Roblox-specific observational study; no Avocado ranking or badge changed.

## 180. 追加調査：creator-community moderationは公開作品だけでなくアイデア共有の場にも関係（2026-10-18 / Security、RQ14 governance）

Zhang et al.'s peer-reviewed [CHI PLAY 2024 study](https://doi.org/10.1145/3677076) used inductive thematic analysis of 2,900 Reddit threads and 61,510 comments from `r/Robloxgamedev` (Sept. 2019–Aug. 2022). It reports three risky community patterns: creators collectively ideating potentially harmful game designs, negotiating boundaries in platform rules, and sharing moderation-evasion strategies. This is an observational study of one Roblox creator community, not Rockstar/Cfx, GTA VI, Avocado or proof that every creator forum has this behavior. It does highlight that safety issues can arise in collaborative design discussion as well as in the published artifact.

Avocado candidate: if an already-authorized creator/Tool community is opened, model the forum, examples, templates and API—not just the submitted package—as separate moderation surfaces. Give contributors short, scoped policy guidance before publishing, contextual report/escalation paths, an appeal/clarification route, and test whether legitimate design questions can proceed without exposing bypass instructions or silently blocking benign work. Measure harmful-content exposure, report resolution, mistaken removals, repeat violations and contributor understanding. This is a governance hypothesis, not a decision to launch Avocado community channels or add moderation systems; no policy or service changed.

## 181. 追加調査：game communityの反応は市場代表性を確かめてから製品判断に使う（2026-10-18 / business pilot、RQ14 demand evidence）

Allen, Bremner & McDonald's [Academy of Management Journal study](https://doi.org/10.5465/amj.2024.0178) was published online April 9, 2026. It combines archival data from 432 Steam Early Access PC games with 29 developer/executive/user interviews. The authors report that incorporating community feedback is associated with better post-launch commercial outcomes when the Early Access participant mix resembles Steam's broader user distribution; in unrepresentative communities, including large ones, greater feedback incorporation is associated with lower post-launch revenue despite more positive ratings within the community. The work concerns one PC games marketplace and commercial outcomes, not Avocado's product/market, and does not mean enthusiastic users' bug reports or accessibility needs should be disregarded.

Avocado candidate: in any future pilot, tag each observation by acquisition path, usage intensity, role, device/region and prior experience; compare vocal/returning contributors to invited and non-responding users and intended target distribution. Separate dependable defect/safety evidence from preference/demand votes; report denominators, attrition and non-response before claiming broad market fit. Invite quieter and less-experienced cohorts through accessible alternative channels. This is pilot-method guidance only, not a reason to launch or delay a product; no market study, recruiting, survey or feature decision changed.


## 182. 追加調査：小規模ゲーム制作者のplaytestは参加者募集とデータ処理がボトルネック（2026-10-18 / Business Pilot、RQ14 evidence quality）

Denisova, Bromley, Mirza-Babaei & Mekler's peer-reviewed [CHI PLAY 2024 study](https://doi.org/10.1145/3677108) interviewed 13 indie-game professionals about game user research and playtesting. The paper identifies finding appropriate participants and handling playtest data among the reported challenges, then discusses adapting established user-research practices to make them more accessible. This is a small qualitative study of indie developers, not Rockstar, GTA VI, Avocado, or an estimate of market demand; it does not claim that every playtest needs a large sample or specialized lab.

Avocado candidate: for any separately authorized RQ14 usability/pilot study, write a short test question, participant-fit criteria, minimally necessary capture plan, retention/deletion rule, and feedback-triage owner before recruiting. Separate observed task outcomes from researcher interpretation and feature requests; use a low-cost, repeatable protocol, disclose recording, and give participants a non-recorded path where feasible. Evaluate recruitment yield, missing-data rate, analysis time and unresolved findings instead of treating more sessions as automatically better. This is a future pilot-method hypothesis only; no participants were recruited and no Avocado study or data capture occurred.

## 183. 追加調査：ゲーム内のAI起因エラーは作中の嘘と誤情報を区別しにくくする（2026-10-18 / AI06、RQ14 trust UX）

Yin et al.'s peer-reviewed [CHI 2024 study](https://doi.org/10.1145/3613904.3642043) used a short 2D RPG, AlphaBetaCity, whose NPC dialogue included human-written lies, prompted LLM-written statements and unintended model errors. In a qualitative user study of 29 adults, participants often began with trust toward NPCs and tried to verify claims in the game world; inconsistent false claims without an apparent narrative purpose could feel like a bug or unfinished writing. The paper also reports participants' trust and character attachment could decrease after they learned dialogue was AI-written. This is one experimental game and recruited sample, not GTA VI evidence, an Avocado evaluation, or a general finding that all users reject AI.

Avocado candidate: for any authorized assistant or creator workflow, distinguish established product facts, sourced claims, model-generated inference and unknown state in the response itself; attach source/date for factual claims, provide a practical way to check or correct errors, and never turn a fluent answer into an entitlement/action success signal. Evaluate whether people can identify which claims are verified versus generated and can recover from an incorrect suggestion; test disclosure wording by audience/context rather than assuming a badge alone creates calibrated trust. This cross-checks RockstarOS's existing non-trusted planner and Broker boundary; no model, disclosure rule, gameplay feature or product claim changed.

## 184. 追加調査：AI能力の開示効果は役割と熟練度で変わる（2026-10-18 / AI06、trust calibration）

Chua, Wang & Yao's [arXiv paper](https://arxiv.org/abs/2503.15514) contrasts a superhuman StarCraft II opponent with a cooperative information assistant. The authors report that revealing capability affected perceived fairness/trust differently across expertise groups and domains; in the assistant scenario, their novice personas tended toward more trust after disclosure, while the effect differed for experts. The paper combines a persona-based interaction study with a 32-person StarCraft II experiment; its findings are context-bound and the record is a preprint, not evidence about GTA VI, Avocado, or every user. The authors caution that disclosure is not a universal fix and that overreliance can still occur.

Avocado candidate: test capability explanations by specific role (planner, answer source, executor), actual permissions and audience familiarity. Measure whether users can predict what the component can do, identify what it cannot authorize, and detect a wrong recommendation—not just whether they noticed an AI label or report higher trust. Preserve user's approval and Broker's independent authorization even when an assistant is marketed as highly capable. This is a test hypothesis aligned with existing LLM/Broker architecture, not a new policy or trust claim; no configuration or permission changed.

## 185. 追加調査：ゲームのaccessibility preferenceは診断名ではなく必要機能で表し、実動作を検証（2026-10-18 / AI06、device/accessibility UX）

May et al.'s peer-reviewed [2026 Frontiers in Computer Science study](https://doi.org/10.3389/fcomp.2026.1823366) proposes Access Profiles as a communication framework between players with disabilities and game developers. Its qualitative work involved interviews/probes with nine disabled players and five indie game developers. Players could state feature preferences such as captions or high contrast, rather than share a disability diagnosis; the authors say this can aid discovery and configuration across games. They explicitly caution that store tags/profile integration do not ensure the setting works correctly, and describe the framework as an evolving process rather than certification. This is an early research framework, not an Avocado feature or GTA VI support evidence.

Avocado candidate: where an already-approved multi-device experience stores accessibility preferences, prefer purpose-specific requested settings over unnecessary diagnosis labels; make each preference optional, scoped, reviewable and deletable, with clear local override. For a Tool/catalog, distinguish provider self-description, interface support, tested behavior on exact client/runtime, and user report; do not translate a tag or profile match into compatibility certification. This requires review against existing consent/profile contracts before any change; no preference, schema, catalog claim or setting was added.

## 186. 追加調査：作者向けTool支援には更新保守と再現できるbug reportも要る（2026-10-18 / RQ14、SDK maintenance）

Lee et al.'s peer-reviewed [Empirical Software Engineering study](https://doi.org/10.1007/s10664-019-09783-w) analyzed 9,521 mods for 20 highly-modded games on Nexus Mods, filtering to items with at least 1,000 endorsements. The authors report that titles with official modding support were associated with higher median endorsement ratios and faster mod releases; across the studied sample, only about 2–4% of submitted bug reports included a code sample or stack trace, and 67% remained in the platform's “New issue” state. These findings are observational and selected to popular mods on one PC platform from data gathered around 2018; they do not show causation, Rockstar/Cfx/GTA VI creator support, Avocado demand, or that every mod needed frequent updates.

Avocado candidate: the existing `systems/rock-star-os/examples/game/owner.py` already emits `rock-game-sdk-diagnostic/1` with authority contact, pending operations/connections, a Wallet snapshot and recovery guidance. Compare that contract and current SDK/Tool release workflow before proposing any expansion: the example diagnoses a local game-exchange sandbox and does not itself demonstrate end-user bug-report intake, game-build compatibility or GTAVI. A future separately approved test could check whether an existing report path can preserve reproducible steps, expected/observed result and exact environment/version while keeping logs optional, consented, bounded and redacted; separate “needs information”, duplicate, fixed and unreproducible states only if the existing workflow has an evidenced gap. Measure reproducibility and resolution time, not report volume. No GTA VI-compatible Tool, bug-report API or new diagnostics behavior was added.

## 187. 追加調査：安全を優先するAI挙動は信頼・再協力意向を上げても、正しい権限判断の証明ではない（2026-10-18 / AI06、trust calibration）

Liu et al.'s peer-reviewed [2025 Human Factors and Ergonomics Society experiment](https://doi.org/10.1177/10711813251357916) used a small interactive game in two between-subjects experiments (N=88 and N=153). A benevolent-assistant condition visibly chose a safer route, healed the player, and sacrificed itself for the final objective; a comparison assistant prioritized its own goal. Both experiments found higher self-reported trust and willingness to use for the benevolent version. The authors note this controlled game may not generalize to real-world assistant use; all players completed the game and could not die, so these results do not establish task reliability, safe permissions, real loss prevention, GTA VI behavior or Avocado efficacy.

Avocado candidate: in any authorized agent study, measure whether safety-prioritizing choices are actually within the user's scope and improve recoverability, alongside trust and return intent. Do not use a reassuring persona, visible “sacrifice,” or high trust score as substitute for least privilege, cost limits, approval, independent result verification and rollback. No persona, permission, approval or execution policy changed.

## 188. 追加調査：UGC公開方式と審査済みrevisionを別状態にする（2026-10-18 / RQ14、catalog governance）

Steamworks' official [Workshop overview](https://partner.steamgames.com/doc/features/workshop?language=english) distinguishes ready-to-use publishing—authors can update items and subscribers receive updates—from a curated queue in which the developer approves each item before distribution. Its [moderation service reference](https://partner.steamgames.com/doc/webapi/IPublishedFileModerationService) further documents a change-number guard: approval fails when an item changed after the moderator fetched the revision, requiring the new version to be reviewed. This is an implementation pattern in Steam's product, not GTA VI's system or Avocado demand evidence.

Avocado candidate: when reviewing existing RQ14 publish/review workflow, check that “author submitted,” “reviewed revision,” “published revision” and “updated revision pending review” are visibly distinct, that a concurrent edit cannot inherit stale approval, and that rollback/withdrawal and appeal have separate owner and state. Popularity/ratings or a prior approval do not attest to a later revision's safety, compatibility or rights. Reuse current workflow state transitions and fixtures if a demonstrated gap exists; no catalog schema, moderation process, gate or game integration changed.

## 189. 追加調査：公式音楽ハブはgame radio preview、standalone single、album商品を分けて案内（2026-10-18 / RQ14 catalog, rights provenance）

Rockstar's [GTA VI music hub](https://www.rockstargames.com/VI/music) explicitly labels selected Leonida radio previews separately from singles on the standalone *Grand Theft Auto VI: The Album*; its launch-trailer article separately calls “Flower in a Hurricane” a new standalone Justice single used in the trailer and says the album is available to pre-save / vinyl or CD preorder, while additional official score information is forthcoming. A song featured in a trailer, a selected radio preview, standalone album availability and an in-game licensed track are therefore different claims and consumer actions. The page and article are promotion/product sources, not a grant of rights for Avocado to host, retransmit or use that audio.

Avocado candidate: maintain media type, use context, rightsholder/license scope, platform, territory/term and availability separately; let sample/preview link to the correct offer without suggesting it includes the full asset or playback rights. Preserve the existing source/rights boundary from §175. No music was copied, streamed, cataloged as an Avocado asset or licensed; no GTA VI capability inferred.

## 190. 追加調査：ローンチ映像の感情的な核は製品仕様の証拠と分ける（2026-10-18 / product UX, evidence labeling）

I replayed the full 1:30 [Rockstar Games Japan launch trailer](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=MNPmV5nWvC4) in the browser. Sampled frames show a man boarding a yacht (about 0:09), Jason and Lucia together in a car (about 0:28), and the pair embracing at sunset (about 1:08), with a quick-cut nightlife/action montage around them, subtitles and the new song. The narrative reads as a relationship-led promotional edit; that interpretation is subjective. It exposes no developer UI, API, permission or feature test; trailer images and text are not proof of accessible controls, exact gameplay behavior, compatibility or service availability.

Avocado candidate: in an approved product demo or onboarding story, lead with one recognizable user outcome, then pair each claim with a nearby, testable status (for example “job saved,” “approval still needed,” “cloud access unavailable here”) and link detail for source, cost and recovery. Test comprehension and task completion separately from emotional response or views. This is a creative direction hypothesis, not validated campaign research; no copy, UI, feature, claim or release gate changed.

## 191. 追加調査：公式ストア案内のsingle-player launch scopeを更新確認（2026-10-18 / RQ14 integration boundary）

Rockstar's June 24, 2026 [pre-order announcement](https://www.rockstargames.com/newswire/article/5171972o3ak5oa/pre-order-grand-theft-auto-vi-on-june-25) explicitly describes GTA VI as a “single player experience” launching November 19 on PS5 and Xbox Series X|S; its current [product page](https://www.rockstargames.com/VI) still lists only those launch platforms. This is the announced launch scope at this search checkpoint. It does not claim Rockstar can never announce a future online mode, creator program, API or licensed integration; continued review of the official news/support/legal pages would be required if that changes.

For AvocadoMini intent #995 and RQ14, the practical stop remains unchanged: no game-side connection can be planned without a published interface or direct permission scoped to exact product/platform/build, data/actions and support. A video, pre-order, other Rockstar title, Cfx/FiveM license or third-party accessory is insufficient. No account was linked, platform contacted, launch product bought, API claimed or integration built.
